### 2026-10-23 S13 claim-token row-order qualification follow-up

Static review of the final migration bodies found that `complete_lock_busy` locks claim-token rows
by generation before its parent record, while recovery batches use `token_id`; token identity uses
`token_id` as its primary key and `(change_id,generation)` as a separate unique key. The candidate
must pre-acquire all applicable namespace-6 keys (including bounded sweep-counter/failure IDs),
then lock parent record rows before token children and order token rows by `token_id ASC`. The
possible token-token wait cycle is source-derived and has not been reproduced. Qualification
scenario `S13-TOKEN-RANK-01` now covers every identified token-first caller and same-scope fence
waits; it remains `NOT_RUN`. No migration or test was run. Evidence: `S13_SOURCE_FINAL_GRANT_AND_LOCK_INVENTORY_2026-10-22.md`,

`docs/commits/2026-10-23-s13-token-lock-order-qualification.md`. Owner: operator accountable,
Codex executor; functional/E2E roles own the later scenario execution. Next: reconcile every
remaining granted API/helper/trigger/conflict path against the same FK-derived order before the
source/design freeze and subsequent functional/E2E phase.

### 2026-10-24 transaction-entry proof follow-up

The independent architecture re-review found that one-scope-per-function alone did not prevent one SQL transaction from invoking multiple versioned mutation functions or from taking namespace 6 before the first entry. The candidate proof now requires exactly one granted mutation entry per transaction and rejects entry if the current backend already holds an advisory lock, has an assigned XID/row-locking write, has a blocking `change_ledger` relation lock, or uses isolation other than `READ COMMITTED`; the design explicitly allows harmless type-resolution `AccessShareLock` and a read-committed non-locking read. It also fixes multi-key acquisition order by the actual signed advisory tuple within every namespace, includes base-slot/hash-collision contention, and corrects the checkpoint identity sequence as table-global/non-transactional. The `PostgresAdmissionStore`/`AdmissionStoreError` candidate allowlists now include stable `P0001 MUTATION_TRANSACTION_INVALID` for this refusal. Qualification cases were added and remain `NOT_RUN`. The final architecture recheck found no new logical issue in these assumptions, while confirming they remain candidate design only. Evidence: `S13_SCOPE_FENCE_IMPLEMENTATION_DESIGN_2026-10-22.md` §“Candidate wait-for proof obligations — 2026-10-24”; `S12_S13_DISPOSABLE_QUALIFICATION_PREREQUISITES_2026-10-23.md` §§“S13 single-mutation transaction and same-namespace ordering refinements — 2026-10-24” and “S13 clean-entry guard scenarios — 2026-10-24”; `src/erp_mcp_private_writer/postgres_store.py` and `service.py` stable-code allowlists. permanently reserves `call_id` and a positive per-change `dispatch_generation` at begin-apply;
abandoned intents burn both identifiers but cannot authorize dispatch. Permit ID/digest are created
only after checkpoint read-back in a versioned issuance transaction, which persists and returns a

qualification explicitly tests abandoned intent, rollback, and lost-response cases. Key revocation
now gets server-assigned non-backdatable time and is ordered by locks/commit visibility, not by a
caller timestamp. Terminalization fails closed unless it uses a fresh READ COMMITTED transaction,
sorts shared key locks, and performs a separate post-lock revocation read; qualification requires
both lock winners and rejects other isolation levels, including a pre-wait stale-snapshot setup.
Evidence: schema design §§32–52 and disposable prerequisites §31. No tests/checks/migrations or DB
operations ran. Next: review the exact revised design again; then proceed only with independent
remaining source mapping, not migration preparation until no valid blocker remains.

2026-10-24 COM-entry race correction: `dispatch_attempts.com_entry_authorized_at` and the revoked-unless-private-writer `private_writer_begin_com_entry_v1` candidate now provide a durable one-way CAS immediately before the injected writer call. `PrivateWriterService` calls the new `AdmissionStore.begin_com_entry` after `mark_invoking` and refuses to call the writer when the CAS loses or its result is unknown. The trigger allows only `NULL → timestamp` on a still-possibly-executing attempt; it preserves that timestamp through later terminalization. Thus a recovery no-entry terminalization must race this row under the same CAS: if entry authorization commits first, no-entry recovery must refuse and keep the slot held; if terminalization wins first, a stale process cannot authorize COM entry. A takeover after authorization still treats the call as possibly executing and blocks readiness until exact recovery; no database transaction spans COM. This closes the specific stale-process entry race in source design, but the absent `record_recovered_not_invoked_v1` still must enforce it, and the candidate is unparsed, unreviewed and unqualified. A lost COM-entry response deliberately leaves the call unresolved. No tests, SQL parser, migration, database operation or COM call ran.

2026-10-25 S13 admission/outcome snapshot consistency guard: the PostgreSQL adapter and coordinator boundaries now reject state/identifier contradictions. `admit` verifies the returned immutable identity, refusal shape, non-empty trimmed owner/evidence references, requires a new admission to be `ACCEPTED_NOT_INVOKED` and owned by the current service instance with no evidence reference, and requires a terminal result to include evidence. Outcome lookup requires a dispatch CAS for accepted states, allows no CAS/evidence/owner on `NOT_ACCEPTED`, requires terminal evidence, and permits a physical-process owner only for `POSSIBLY_EXECUTING`. Startup inventory applies the same owner/evidence shape rules, rejects pending calls bound to another physical-base fingerprint or writer host, and rejects duplicate call IDs, dispatch CAS IDs, or barrier IDs. These rules match migration 027's `private_writer_admit_v1`, `private_writer_lookup_v1`, and per-base startup contract. The barrier decoder also refuses `QUIESCED` snapshots that retain unresolved call IDs. This is source-only hardening, not SQL or runtime proof. `service.py` SHA-256: `E3D1F562EED36EA696E158E26233C5E5EFD5AB8667820A20C010300BC1D4F263`; `postgres_store.py` SHA-256: `36C0CC5FB0C1ACC744F945A9520DC7F0D64A2003E8148ABA8DE96121E4DF8E7D`. 2026-10-25 S13 checkpoint-before-permit ordering correction: `DispatchGate.issue_permit` now resolves and validates the immutable dispatch intent and any existing permit binding before writing a checkpoint. A mismatched/missing call intent therefore refuses before ledger-head work or external sink writes. An exact retry with an existing immutable permit binding reuses its stored checkpoint/signing tuple and avoids creating a second checkpoint; a first issuance still writes/read-backs and records the checkpoint before the versioned permit routine. It now also uses `record_checkpoint`'s returned internal `checkpoints.seq` for the permit digest, while using the ledger event sequence only to identify and validate the signed checkpoint. This matches `issue_permit` in migrations 007/015 and `private_writer_issue_permit_v1`; the two sequence domains are distinct. Same-intent concurrent issue attempts, crash windows, and checkpoint-store idempotency remain for designated functional/E2E/database review. Current whole-file `checkpoint.py` SHA-256: `0A1E24DFB4DDD4613624A06F351B36BBB447502D00DC9513ED8D51F92E2F1096`.
